## Examples

cplot_xy variable=potential z=50. 

## 23.183 cplot_xyz

<table><tr><td>parameter</td><td>data type</td><td>values [defaults]</td></tr><tr><td colspan="3"></td></tr><tr><td>variable</td><td>char</td><td>(see list)</td></tr><tr><td>data_file</td><td>char</td><td></td></tr><tr><td>wave_option</td><td>char</td><td>[right],total,left</td></tr><tr><td>xy_from</td><td>realx2</td><td></td></tr><tr><td>xy_to</td><td>realx2</td><td></td></tr><tr><td>xrange</td><td>realx2</td><td></td></tr><tr><td>yrange</td><td>realx2</td><td></td></tr><tr><td>z_min</td><td>real</td><td>[-1.e9]</td></tr><tr><td>z_max</td><td>real</td><td>[1.e9]</td></tr><tr><td>mode_index</td><td>intg</td><td>[1]</td></tr><tr><td>grid_sizes</td><td>intgx2</td><td>[20, 20]</td></tr><tr><td>level</td><td>intg</td><td>[10]</td></tr><tr><td>trap_index</td><td>intg</td><td>[1]</td></tr></table>

cplot_xyz is a post-processor statement used to plot structural data on a 2D plane. Unlike plot_2d, this command can only generate contour plots. 

Other related commands exist and should be used depending on the 2D/3D nature of the original simulation results. The following rules apply: 

• 2D simulations: use plot_2d 

• 3D cylindrical simulations with one mesh plane: use plot_2d 

• xy plane from a 3D simulation: use cplot_xy for contour plots of scalar variables, splot_xy for 3D surface plots of scalar variables or vplot_xy for vector variables. 

• xyz plane from a 3D simulation: use cplot_xyz for contour plots of scalar variables, splot_xyz for 3D surface plots of scalar variables or vplot_xyz for vector variables. 

## Parameters

This statement is similar to cplot_xy and varies only in the way it defines the plotting plane. As such, most of the parameters are also similar to those in plot_2d. 

• xy_from and xy_to define the $\scriptstyle ( \mathbf { x } , \mathbf { y } )$ corners of the plotting plane. 

• z_min and z_max define the z coordinates of the corners of the plotting plane. 

## Examples

cplot_xyz variable=wave_intensity xy_from=(0.5, 0.0) xy_to=(0.5 3.0) && grid_sizes=(60, 20) 

## 23.184 csuprem_mask

<table><tr><td>parameter</td><td>data type</td><td>values [defaults]</td></tr><tr><td>tag</td><td>char</td><td>[void]</td></tr></table>

csuprem_mask is used in the .layer file pre-processing in conjunction with the export_layers_to_suprem command. It modifies the CSUPREM input files that are generated by that command and adds a comment line at the appropriate place. An optional used-defined label can also be added as part of the comment line to facilitate the identification of layers. 

## Parameters

tag is a user-defined label that can be added to the comment line in CSUPREM 

## 23.185 current_conc

<table><tr><td>parameter</td><td>data type</td><td>values [defaults]</td></tr><tr><td colspan="3"></td></tr><tr><td>data_file</td><td>char</td><td>[void]</td></tr><tr><td>use_macro</td><td>char</td><td>[no]</td></tr><tr><td>fit_outfile</td><td>char</td><td>[void]</td></tr><tr><td>auto_pn_ratio</td><td>char</td><td>[no]</td></tr><tr><td>conc_log_scale</td><td>char</td><td>[no]</td></tr><tr><td>conc_range</td><td>realx2</td><td>[(1.e23 1.e24)] (<eq>m^{-3}</eq>)</td></tr><tr><td>pn_ratio</td><td>real</td><td>[1.]</td></tr><tr><td>av_index</td><td>real</td><td>[3.3]</td></tr><tr><td>auger_n</td><td>real</td><td>[2.e-42] (<eq>m^{6}/s</eq>)</td></tr><tr><td>auger_p</td><td>real</td><td>[2.e-42] (<eq>m^{6}/s</eq>)</td></tr><tr><td>life_n</td><td>real</td><td>[1.e-6] (sec.)</td></tr><tr><td>life_p</td><td>real</td><td>[1.e-6] (sec.)</td></tr><tr><td>data_point</td><td>intg</td><td>[30]</td></tr><tr><td>well_num</td><td>intg</td><td>[1]</td></tr><tr><td>zseg_num</td><td>intg</td><td>[1]</td></tr></table>

The statement current_conc is a gain preview statement which allows the user to find the relation between carrier concentration and the recombination current in the quantum well active region. The recombination rate is expressed as 

$$
R = R _ {s p o n} + R _ {a u g e r} + n / t a u _ {n} + p / t a u _ {p}
$$

where the spontaneous emission spectrum computed from the quantum well model is integrated over all emission wavelengths and the Auger recombination is given by auger_n and auger_p, defined below. The last two terms are due to traps, surface recombination, thermal leakage, etc. 

## Parameters

• data_file is the file to which the graphic data is written in ASCII format. 

• use_macro directs the program to use the material parameters such as Auger coeficients from the material macros instead from parameters in this statement. If this parameter is set to yes, parameters of auger_n, auger_p, life_n and life_p in this statement will not be used for the model fitting. 

• fit_outfile if set to a valid file name, is used to direct the model fitting results to a file in the working directory. 

auto_pn_ratio is used to indicate if automatic setting of hole/electron density ratio is used in PICS3D simulation. If positive, averaged hole/electron density from the drift-difusion solver is used. When this setting is enabled, the value defined in pn_ratio is ignored. 

• conc_range is the electron concentration range in the well. 

• pn_ratio is the ratio of hole over electron concentrations. Note that this ratio can be set to an arbitrary number in the gain preview. In the main solver, however, this ratio is determined by the simulator automatically, according to the local Fermi levels. 

• conc_log_scale would vary the concentration in log scale. 

• av_index is the estimated average refractive index. 

• auger_n is the Auger coeficient $C _ { n }$ . 

• auger_p is the Auger coeficient $C _ { p }$ . 

• life_n is the minority carrier life time for electrons. 

• life_p is the minority carrier life time for holes . 

data_point is the number of data points to be used in the current versus concentration plot. 

• well_num is the total number of active quantum wells involved. 

• zseg_num is the z-segment number. 

## 23.186 cylindrical

<table><tr><td>parameter</td><td>data type</td><td>values [defaults]</td></tr><tr><td>axis</td><td>char</td><td>x,[y]</td></tr><tr><td>cylindrical_origin</td><td>real</td><td>[0.] (μm)</td></tr><tr><td>min_core</td><td>real</td><td>[0] (μm)</td></tr></table>

The cylindrical statement is used to define a cylindrical coordinate system. It has been implemented so that 3D devices with cylindrical symmetry can be modeled with a single mesh plane. 

With this setting, the 2D cut of the device shown in the .layer file corresponds to a radial cut. 3D results are obtained by integrating the 2D results of the simulation from 0 to 2π. Note that in versions prior to 2012, the integration range was from 0 to π and a scaling factor had to be used during plotting. This scaling is no longer necessary. 

Please refer to Sec. 6.2 for details. 

## Parameters

• axis is the cartesian axis which is used as the rotation or z-axis of the cylindrical coordinate system. The other direction is automatically equivalent to r. 

• cylindrical_origin is used to ofset r axis values during the conversion to the cylindrical coordinate system. A value larger than zero turns a rectangular 2D cut into a torus rather than a cylinder. 

• core_min is a minimum r value used when converting the local mesh area into a volume for the FEM. This value is necessary to avoid division by zero errors since the gradient operator in cylindrical coordinates contains a term in 1 ∂ r ∂θ 

## 23.187 dbr_truncate

<table><tr><td>parameter</td><td>data type</td><td>values [defaults]</td></tr><tr><td>dir</td><td>char</td><td>[left] right</td></tr><tr><td>kappa_l</td><td>real</td><td>[5.]</td></tr><tr><td>sec_num</td><td>intg</td><td>[1]</td></tr></table>

dbr_truncate is used to modify the DBR grating in edge type of lasers so that numerical stability may be achieved. When a DBR grating structure is used as a reflector for a laser diode, the propagating light can only penetrate a finite distance before fully reflected. Such a penetration length is usually in the order of several 1/kappa, where kappa is the coupling coeficient. The DBR gratings beyond this penetration length does not contribute to the laser diode operation but may cause numerical stability during Newton iterations. Thus, we artificially truncate the DBR portion beyond the penetration length by setting the kappa there to zero. 

## Parameters

• dirdir is the direction towards which the DBR grating is used as a reflector. For example, dir=left means the DBR is used to reflect light coming from the left. 

• kappa_l is a real number of around five. It is used to measure the penetration length beyond which the DBR is to be truncated. The penetration length is given by kappa_l/kappa, where kappa is the coupling coeficient. 

• sec_num is the index of the section where the DBR truncation is applicable. 

Examples 

dbr_truncate sec_num=2 kappa_l=5 

## 23.188 define_alias

<table><tr><td>parameter</td><td>data type</td><td>values [defaults]</td></tr><tr><td>alias</td><td>char</td><td>[void]</td></tr><tr><td>name</td><td>real</td><td>[void]</td></tr></table>

define_alias is used to define an alias which may be used instead of the original variable in all input files. It acts in many ways like the #define directive in C/C++. To associate an alias to a numerical value, use define_symbol instead. 

## Parameters

• alias is the alias being used. 

• name is the name the alias represents. 

## Examples

define_alias alias=Vg name=voltage_2 

This defines Vg (gate voltage) as being equivalent to voltage_2. 

## 23.189 define_cavity

<table><tr><td>parameter</td><td>data type</td><td>values [defaults]</td></tr><tr><td>propagation_dir</td><td>char</td><td>y, [z]</td></tr><tr><td>symmetric_x</td><td>char</td><td>[yes], no</td></tr><tr><td>import_xy_wave</td><td>char</td><td></td></tr><tr><td>eim_core_xrange</td><td>realx2</td><td>(μm)</td></tr><tr><td>eim_core_zrange</td><td>realx2</td><td>(μm)</td></tr><tr><td>eim_core_index</td><td>real</td><td>[3.5]</td></tr><tr><td>eim_clad_xindex</td><td>real</td><td>[1.5]</td></tr><tr><td>eim_clad_zindex</td><td>real</td><td>[1.5]</td></tr><tr><td>pcsel_coupling_h1</td><td>real</td><td>(m-1)</td></tr><tr><td>pcsel_size_factor</td><td>real</td><td>[1]</td></tr></table>

The define_cavity command is exclusively used in PICS3D and is similar to vcsel_model. It is used to define the optical mode profile of a photonic crystal structure in the cartesian coordinate system; this can be either a nanocavity caused by a gap in the photonic crystal or a full size PCSEL device. 

Unlike vcsel_model, define_cavity does not try to compute the standing wave overlap between the optical mode and the active region. Mode shapes are also imported rather than directly computed. 

## Parameters

• propagation_dir is the overall direction of the wave vector in the laser cavity. 

• symmetric_x is a flag indicating the left border of the cavity is a symmetry axis. This used purely for wave import purposes. 

• import_xy_wave is the name of text file containing a mode profile that should be imported into the simulation. The format should be defined in 3 columns (x y intensity) with x changing the fastest and a line break between each set of y values. A uniform grid is assumed. 

• eim_core_xrange is the waveguide core x-range for efective index method (EIM) when computing the wave profile. If propagation_dir=y, eim_core_zrange serves the same purpose and the defines the depth of the waveguide core. If propagation_dir=z, it is assumed the y-range of the waveguide core is the same as the x-range. 

• eim_core_index is the real part of the refractive index in the waveguide core. Similarly, eim_clad_xindex and eim_clad_zindex respectively define the real part of the refractive index in the cladding for the x and z directions. If propagation_dir=z, it is assumed that the cladding along y is the same as along x. 

• pcsel_coupling_h1 is a surface power extraction coeficient for PCSELs. As this class of device can be classified as being of the same type as a secondorder grating DFB, the radiation loss is given by 2 h . See Sec. 16.8 for a full derivation. 

• pcsel_size_factor is a scaling factor that may be used to account for the symmetry of a PCSEL. Note that this flag is intended to account for symmetry along the propagation direction, whereas symmetric_x can only account for left/right symmetry from the initial geometry. Depending on user input, a scaling factor of up to 4 may be required. 

## Examples

```txt
define_cavity propagation_dir=y &&
eim_core_xrange=(0 0.5) eim_core_zrange=(0. 1.035) &&
eim_core_index=3.5 eim_clad_xindex=1.5 eim_clad_zindex=1. 
```

## 23.190 define_material

<table><tr><td>parameter</td><td>data type</td><td>values [defaults]</td></tr><tr><td>mater</td><td>intg</td><td>[1]</td></tr></table>

This statement is used when a material number is re-used between z-segments. The first z-segment where this material occurrs must use load_macro to load the macro parameters. The other z-segments use define_material to indicate load_macro has already been used. 

## Parameters

• mater is the number of the material being reused. 

## Examples

define_material mater=8 

## 23.191 define_symbol

<table><tr><td>parameter</td><td>data type</td><td>values [defaults]</td></tr><tr><td>symbol</td><td>char</td><td>[void]</td></tr><tr><td>value</td><td>real</td><td></td></tr></table>

define_symbol is used to associate numerical values with symbols. It acts in many ways like the #define directive in C/C++. 

This may be used to simplify input files or to represent repeated numerical values so that revisions are more convenient. 

A few precautions should be taken when using this statement: 

• 1) Avoid using it when the same symbol appears than once within a line; 

• 2) The line containing the symbol must have enough line space so that the total length does not exceed 80 character after value substitution; 

• 3) Please avoid using symbols that are already defined as parameters in crosslight.tab. 

For example, the following statement will cause problems: 

define_symbol symbol=doping value=2.e24 

Since "doping" is commonly used statement, the program will replace all occurrence of "doping" by "2.e24" and the input file will loss its original meaning. To avoid this problem, try 

define_symbol symbol=john_smith_doping value=2.e24 

To associate a symbol to a variable or a string input value, use define_alias instead. 

## Parameters

• symbol is the symbol to be used to represent numerical value in the program. 

• value is the numerical value to be used. 

## Examples

define_symbol symbol=my_doping value=1.e24 

## 23.192 define_vertical_position

<table><tr><td>parameter</td><td>data type</td><td>values [defaults]</td></tr><tr><td>label</td><td>char</td><td>[void]</td></tr><tr><td>reference_to</td><td>char</td><td>[void]</td></tr><tr><td>use_y_coord</td><td>real</td><td>(um)</td></tr><tr><td>above_previous</td><td>real</td><td>(um)</td></tr><tr><td>below_previous</td><td>real</td><td>(um)</td></tr><tr><td>cut_at_x</td><td>real</td><td>(um)</td></tr><tr><td>from_top</td><td>real</td><td>(um)</td></tr><tr><td>from_bottom</td><td>real</td><td>(um)</td></tr><tr><td>above_reference</td><td>real</td><td>(um)</td></tr><tr><td>below_reference</td><td>real</td><td>(um)</td></tr><tr><td>between_materials</td><td>intgx2</td><td></td></tr></table>

define_vertical_position is used in .sol file to label a vertical position for later reference. This can be done in one of the following ways: 

• 1) Use absolute coordinates 

• 2) Defined as first occurrence of material interface between two materials when searching from bottom to top 

• 3) In reference to a previously defined position, i.e., using relative distance 

• 4) Use relative distance with respect to the top or bottom of the x-y mesh. 

## Parameters

• label is a position label at a specific y-coordinate. 

• reference_to is used to define a reference point for approach number 3). It must be a predefined position label. 

• use_y_coord is the y-coordinate for approach number 1). 

• above_previous indicates the position is above a label in the preceeding statement for 3). 

• below_previous indicates the position is below a label in the preceeding statement for 3). 

• cut_at_x is used to define the x-position when using approach 2). 

• from_top defines the distance from top of the mesh system in 4). 

• from_bottom defines the distance from bottom of the mesh system in 4). 

• above_reference is the distance above the reference point defined by parameter reference_to. 

• below_reference is the distance below the reference point defined by parameter reference_to. 

• between_materials defines the two materials used to identify the interface position in 2). 

## Examples

```python
define_vertical_position label=sio2/si && cut_at_x=0. between_materials=(2 3) 
```

This example labels a y-position between materials 2 and 3 and label it as “sio2/si". 

## 23.193 delta_real_index_caxis

delta_real_index_caxis is a passive macro parameter describing the birefringence in wurtzite materials. This value describes the diference between the extraordinary index and the ordinary index with the latter value being defined in real_index. 

To model the birefringence, optical_axis must also be used to configure the waveguide orientation with respect to the extraordinary axis. 

The parameters for this statement are the same as for all other material statements. See material_par in section 23.470 for examples and further details. 

## 23.194 delta_so_bar

delta_so_bar is an active macro parameter defining the spin-orbit coupling (in eV) in a zincblende Hamiltonian. It applies to the barrier region of a quantum well. 

The parameters for this statement are the same as for all other material statements. See material_par in section 23.470 for examples and further details. 

## 23.195 delta_so_well

delta_so_well is an active macro parameter defining the spin-orbit coupling (in eV) in a zincblende Hamiltonian. It applies to quantum wells and bulk active regions. 

The parameters for this statement are the same as for all other material statements. See material_par in section 23.470 for examples and further details. 

## 23.196 delta1_bar

deltaj_bar,j=1..3 are a set of active macro parameters defining valence band edges (in eV) in the barrier of a wurtzite quantum well. They correspond to the $\Delta _ { j }$ values appearing in the Hamiltonian[64]. 

$\Delta _ { 1 }$ corresponds to the crystal field splitting $( \Delta _ { c r } )$ 

• $\Delta _ { 2 }$ and $\Delta _ { 3 }$ correspond to the spin-orbit splitting. It is common to set $\Delta _ { 2 } =$ $\begin{array} { r } { \Delta _ { 3 } = \frac { \Delta _ { s o } } { 3 } } \end{array}$ 

The parameters for this statement are the same as for all other material statements. See material_par in section 23.470 for examples and further details. 

## 23.197 delta2_bar

See delta1_bar. 

## 23.198 delta3_bar

See delta1_bar. 

## 23.199 delta1_bulk

deltaj_bulk,j=1..3 are a set of passive macro parameters defining valence band edges (in $\mathrm { e V } )$ in wurtzite materials. They correspond to the $\Delta _ { j }$ values appearing in the Hamiltonian[64]. 

$\Delta _ { 1 }$ corresponds to the crystal field splitting $( \Delta _ { c r } )$ 

• $\Delta _ { 2 }$ and $\Delta _ { 3 }$ correspond to the spin-orbit splitting. It is common to set $\Delta _ { 2 } =$ $\begin{array} { r } { \Delta _ { 3 } = \frac { \Delta _ { s o } } { 3 } } \end{array}$ 

The parameters for this statement are the same as for all other material statements. See material_par in section 23.470 for examples and further details. 

## 23.200 delta2_bulk

See delta1_bulk. 

## 23.201 delta3_bulk

See delta1_bulk. 

## 23.202 delta1_well

deltaj_well,j=1..3 are a set of active macro parameters defining valence band edges (in eV) for a wurtzite quantum well or bulk active region. They correspond to the $\Delta _ { j }$ values appearing in the Hamiltonian[64]: 

$\Delta _ { 1 }$ corresponds to the crystal field splitting $( \Delta _ { c r } )$ 

• $\Delta _ { 2 }$ and $\Delta _ { 3 }$ correspond to the spin-orbit splitting. It is common to set $\Delta _ { 2 } =$ $\begin{array} { r } { \Delta _ { 3 } = \frac { \Delta _ { s o } } { 3 } } \end{array}$ 

The parameters for this statement are the same as for all other material statements. See material_par in section 23.470 for examples and further details. 

## 23.203 delta2_well

See delta1_well. 

## 23.204 delta3_well

See delta1_well. 

## 23.205 d1_bar

di_bar,i=1...6 are a set of parameters used to define barrier properties in wurtzite quantum well active macros. They define the shear deformation potential (D<sub>i</sub>) as defined in Ref [64]. 

The parameters for this statement are the same as for all other material statements. See material_par in section 23.470 for examples and further details. 

23.206 d2_bar 

See Sec. 23.205. 

23.207 d3_bar 

See Sec. 23.205. 

23.208 d4_bar 

See Sec. 23.205. 

23.209 d5_bar 

See Sec. 23.205. 

## 23.210 d6_bar

See Sec. 23.205. 

## 23.211 d1_bulk

di_bulk,i=1...6 are a set of parameters used to define barrier properties in wurtzite passive macros. They define the shear deformation potential (D<sub>i</sub>) as defined in Ref [64]. 

The parameters for this statement are the same as for all other material statements. See material_par in section 23.470 for examples and further details. 

## 23.212 d2_bulk

See Sec. 23.211. 

## 23.213 d3_bulk

See Sec. 23.211. 

## 23.214 d4_bulk

See Sec. 23.211. 

## 23.215 d5_bulk

di_well,i=1...6 are a set of parameters used in the active macros for wurtzite quantum wells and bulk active regions. They define the shear deformation potential (D<sub>i</sub>) as defined in Ref [64]. 

The parameters for this statement are the same as for all other material statements. See material_par in section 23.470 for examples and further details. 

23.218 d2_well

## 23.223 diagonal_split

<table><tr><td>parameter</td><td>data type</td><td>values [defaults]</td></tr><tr><td>split_dir</td><td>char</td><td>[north-west], north-east</td></tr><tr><td>right_mater_from</td><td>char</td><td>[east], west, north, south, original</td></tr><tr><td>right_doping_from</td><td>char</td><td>[east], west, north, south, original</td></tr></table>

The diagonal_split command is used in the .layer file to split a rectangle into two: a line drawn between two corners splits the rectangle into two pieces which can have diferent material or doping properties from the original rectangle. 

In this command, cardinal directions from a map are used instead of the usual up/down/left/right convention. 

## Parameters

• split_dir gives the direction of the diagonal line splitting the rectangle. 

• right_mater_from is used to initialize the material number of the new triangle created from the split. The triangle left of the split line is left unchanged and uses the material number of the original rectangle. The triangle to the right of the split line may borrow the material number from one of the neighbors of the original rectangle or keep its original number. 

• right_doping_from is the same as right_mater_from but deals with the doping value used in the new triangle to the right of the split line. 

## 23.224 dielectric_constant

The material statement dielectric_constant defines the static dielectric constant which appears in the Poisson’s equation. It does not afect the wave equation which is written for optical frequencies so this value is unrelated to the value of real_index. 

The parameters for this statement are the same as for all other material statements. See material_par in section 23.470 for examples and further details. 

## 23.225 diferential_gain

<table><tr><td>parameter</td><td>data type</td><td>values [defaults]</td></tr><tr><td colspan="3"></td></tr><tr><td>data_file</td><td>char</td><td></td></tr><tr><td>include_data</td><td>char</td><td></td></tr><tr><td>conc_log_scale</td><td>char</td><td>[no], yes</td></tr><tr><td>wavel_range</td><td>realx2</td><td>(μm)</td></tr><tr><td>conc_range</td><td>realx2</td><td>[1.e23 1.e24] (<eq>m^{-3}</eq>)</td></tr><tr><td>pn_ratio</td><td>real</td><td>[1]</td></tr><tr><td>av_index</td><td>real</td><td>[3.3]</td></tr><tr><td>data_point</td><td>intg</td><td></td></tr></table>

diferential_gain plots the diferential material gain (at the peak of the gain curve) versus carrier density in the active region. This statement can be used in the gain preview module. 

## Parameters

• data_file specifies a file name used to save a copy of the plot data. 

• include_data includes data files from other gain calculations on the plot for comparison purposes. 

• wavel_range is the wavelength range used to search for the gain peak. 

• conc_range is the electron concentration range in the well used for the plot. 

• pn_ratio is the ratio of hole over electron concentrations. 

Note that this ratio is set to an arbitrary number in the gain preview in order to generate a 1-dimensional plot or a family of spectral curves which depend on a single parameter. In the main solver, this ratio is normally unused and the gain calculations automatically make use of the local Fermi levels for both electrons and holes. 

• av_index is the estimated average refractive index. 

• data_point is the number of data points in the curve. 

• conc_log_scale determines if the carrier density points are spaced linearly or on a logarithmic scale. 

## Examples

differential_gain wavel_range=(1.0 1.4) && conc_range=(5.e23 5.e24) pn_ratio=1 data_point=20 

## 23.226 difuse

<table><tr><td>parameter</td><td>data type</td><td>values [defaults]</td></tr><tr><td>time</td><td>real</td><td>[1.] (min)</td></tr><tr><td>nstep</td><td>intg</td><td>[20]</td></tr></table>

difuse solves a simplified impurity difusion model before the thermal equilibrium solution is used to initialize the carrier drift-difusion model. 

For a given impurity h and its local concentration $S _ { h } ,$ , the difusion process is represented by the following equation: 

$$
\nabla \cdot D _ {h} \overrightarrow {\nabla S _ {h}} = \frac {S _ {h}}{\tau_ {h}} + \frac {d S _ {h}}{d t}
$$

where $D _ { h }$ is the difusion constant and $\tau _ { h }$ is the difusion lifetime. 

At the interface between two materials (1  2), boundary conditions are represented by the following segregation equation: 

$$
\overrightarrow {J _ {h 1 2}} = \overrightarrow {v _ {h}} \left(S _ {h 1} - S _ {h 2} \frac {S S _ {2}}{S S _ {1}}\right)
$$

where $J _ { h }$ is the net impurity flux across the interface, $v _ { h }$ is a velocity term, and SS is the solid solubility of the impurity in each material. 

The temperature at which the difusion takes place is set by the preceding temperature statement, which should then be re-issued to set the temperature for the thermal equilibrium solution. 

This command works in conjunction with the add_difuse_impurity, dif_coef and segregation 

## Parameters

• time is the time (in minutes) over which the difusion takes place. 

• nstep is the number of time steps used to subdivide the above interval. 

## Examples

```txt
temperature temp=700
diffuse time=10.0 nstep=100
temperature temp=300 
```

This describes a ten minute difusion step at 700 degrees Celsius. The temperature is then reset to 300 degrees Celsius in order to solve the thermal equilibrium solution. 

## 23.227 dif_coef

<table><tr><td>parameter</td><td>data type</td><td>values [defaults]</td></tr><tr><td>impurity</td><td>char</td><td></td></tr><tr><td>mater_label</td><td>char</td><td></td></tr><tr><td>a_fac</td><td>real</td><td>[1.e-6] (<eq>m^{2}/s</eq>)</td></tr><tr><td>e_act</td><td>real</td><td>[0.5] (eV)</td></tr><tr><td>life_diff</td><td>real</td><td>[1.e6] (s)</td></tr><tr><td>mater</td><td>intg</td><td>[1]</td></tr></table>

dif_coef sets the parameters of the difusion equation in the difuse command. The difusion constant in this command is defined using a standard Arrhenius form: D = Ae−f racEkBT. 

## Parameters

• impurity sets the difusion parameters for one particular impurity defined by add_difuse_impurity. 

• mater is the host material number in which the specified difusion parameters apply. This host material may also be identified by a label in mater_label. 

• a_fac is the exponential prefactor used to define the difusion constant. 

• e_act is the activation energy used to define the difusion constant. 

• life_dif is the difusion lifetime. 

## Examples

diff_coef impurity=hydrogen a_fac=1.e-7 e_act=0.6 

## 23.228 direct_eigen

<table><tr><td>parameter</td><td>data type</td><td>values [defaults]</td></tr><tr><td>select_modes</td><td>char</td><td>[no] yes</td></tr><tr><td>max_index</td><td>char</td><td>[yes] no</td></tr><tr><td>max_element</td><td>char</td><td>[no] yes</td></tr><tr><td>sigma_factor</td><td>real</td><td>[2.]</td></tr><tr><td>select_index</td><td>real</td><td></td></tr><tr><td>arnoldi_base</td><td>intg</td><td>[10]</td></tr></table>

The statement direct_eigen is used to activate the restarted Arnoldi algorithm for the optical mode solver. 

This statement can be used in conjunction with multimode if multiple lateral modes are present. 

## Application Notes

direct_eigen is not recommended for use in structures without lateral variation. If the mode shape has no variation along the x direction, then the eigensolver should expect to find one degenerate mode for each vertical $x = x _ { i }$ mesh line. In theory, picking any one of these degenerate modes is suficient as they have the same shape. 

In practice however, lack of numerical precision can result in nearly-degenerate modes which are identical in the y direction but difer along x. Picking a single mode out of these nearly-degenerate modes can therefore result in an artificial lateral variation in the mode intensity. Considering ALL of the degenerate modes for the simulation works around this issue as the sum of all modes respects the expected uniform lateral behavior; however, this approach slows down the simulation. 

direct_eigen is also not recommended for broad-area devices. The situation here is similar to the above case but even inclusion of all degenerate modes is not suficient to capture the physics of interest. For broad-area devices, the lateral standing wave pattern is often of interest but the mesh density in the x direction is seldom dense enough to capture this efect. 

In these two cases, using the optical_field statement instead of direct_eigen is recommended. Note that these two commands should not be used together. 

## Parameters

• select_modes , if positive, is used to select eigen modes based on an efective index value supplied through parameter select_index. The eigen modes found will have efective indices below select_index. If negative, the search of eigen value will start from efective index estimated from the highest local material index within the solution region. 

• max_index turns on automatic mode search based on a maximum material index of the device structure. 

• max_element , if positive, instructs the simulator to find eigen modes based on maximum value of matrix elements. 

• sigma_factor is used in conjunction with max_element to obtain an estimate of the maximum eigen value. This factor is multiplied with the maximum matrix element to provide a starting point for eigen value search. 

• arnoldi_base is used to control the number of Arnoldi eigen solutions we intend to work with. The total number of eigen vectors is this number plus the number of modes we specify in the multimode. 

## Examples

direct_eigen sigma_factor=2. 

## 23.229 disconnect_zmesh

<table><tr><td>parameter</td><td>data type</td><td>values [defaults]</td></tr><tr><td>plane</td><td>intg</td><td>[-9999]</td></tr><tr><td>zseg_num</td><td>intg</td><td>[-9999]</td></tr></table>

This statement serves the same purpose as the z_connect=no option in the 3d_solution_method statement: it forces all connecting elements between certain z planes to zero so 

that no current can flow between them. This statement ofers a bit more control as it allows disconnection of individual planes and segments instead of turning of all connections at once. 

## Parameters

• plane is a plane number. The connection will be cut between plane and plane+1. 

• zseg_num is a segment number. The connection will be cut between the last plane of zseg_num and the first plane of zseg_num+1, thereby disconnecting the two segments. 

## Examples

disconnect_zmesh zseg_num=2 

## 23.230 do_raytrace_3d

<table><tr><td>parameter</td><td>data type</td><td>values [defaults]</td></tr><tr><td>3d_emission_model</td><td>char</td><td>[plane]</td></tr><tr><td>external_package</td><td>char</td><td>[void]</td></tr><tr><td>working_direction</td><td>char</td><td>[-y]</td></tr><tr><td>device_kind</td><td>char</td><td>[led]</td></tr><tr><td>incident_light_side</td><td>char</td><td>[+y]</td></tr><tr><td>surface_model</td><td>char</td><td>[void]</td></tr><tr><td>surface_model_side</td><td>char</td><td>[+y]</td></tr><tr><td>reject_edges</td><td>char</td><td>[no]</td></tr><tr><td>extract_to_file</td><td>char</td><td>[void]</td></tr><tr><td>extract_data</td><td>char</td><td>[wavelength_absorption]</td></tr><tr><td>append_data</td><td>char</td><td>[no]</td></tr><tr><td>dome_bottom_mirror</td><td>char</td><td>[yes]</td></tr><tr><td>precision</td><td>real</td><td>[0.01](percentage)</td></tr><tr><td>te_portion</td><td>real</td><td>[0.5]</td></tr><tr><td>tm_portion</td><td>real</td><td>[0.5]</td></tr><tr><td>plate_thickness</td><td>real</td><td>[100.] (um)</td></tr><tr><td>plate_width</td><td>real</td><td>[1000.] (um)</td></tr><tr><td>dome_cyl_height</td><td>real</td><td>[500.] (um)</td></tr><tr><td>dome_cyl_radius</td><td>real</td><td>[400.] (um)</td></tr><tr><td>dome_sph_radius</td><td>real</td><td>[400.] (um)</td></tr><tr><td>dome_base_thickness</td><td>real</td><td>[10.] (um)</td></tr><tr><td>scale_refl</td><td>real</td><td>[1.]</td></tr><tr><td>scale_tran</td><td>real</td><td>[1.]</td></tr><tr><td>thickness1</td><td>real</td><td>[0.023]</td></tr><tr><td>thickness2</td><td>real</td><td>[0.034]</td></tr><tr><td>package_refr_index</td><td>realx2</td><td>[1.7,1.e-7]</td></tr><tr><td>effective_exit_index</td><td>realx2</td><td>[1.0,0.0]</td></tr><tr><td>refr_index1</td><td>realx2</td><td>[1.3,0.0]</td></tr><tr><td>refr_index2</td><td>realx2</td><td>[1.7,0.0]</td></tr><tr><td>datafile_range</td><td>intgx2</td><td>[1 1000]</td></tr><tr><td>initial_rays</td><td>intg</td><td>[5000]</td></tr><tr><td>max_secondary</td><td>intg</td><td>[100000]</td></tr><tr><td>3d_angle_store</td><td>intg</td><td>[50]</td></tr><tr><td>emit_points_number</td><td>intg</td><td>[10]</td></tr><tr><td>max_primary</td><td>intg</td><td>[5000]</td></tr><tr><td>n_layer</td><td>intg</td><td>[1]</td></tr><tr><td>surface_power_grid</td><td>intg2</td><td>[100,100]</td></tr><tr><td>TE_TM_mode</td><td>intg</td><td>[0]</td></tr></table>

do_raytrace_3d is used to set parameters for and to activate the raytracing module for 3D simulation of light emitting diodes (LED). 

It can also be used for 2D simulations. In this case, the x-y modeling plane is extruded in the z direction with a thickness of 1 μm to create a 3D volume compatible with the 3D raytracing program. However, emitted rays are restricted to the original 2D plane. 

## Parameters

• 3d_emission_model is the emission source model of an LED active region. It can take one of the following values: 

– one_point: the spontaneous emission power is averaged to find the center of emission. All the rays emanate from this one point. 

– xy_plane: the spontaneous emission power is averaged into a few “hot spots” of emission. Each of these spots is allowed to emit rays which carry the total integrated power of that spot. This is the recommended model when the quantum well plane normal is along the z-axis. 

– xz_plane: same as xy_plane except for the plane of averaging for the “hot spots”. This is the recommended model when the z-axis is in the plane of the quantum well. 

– mesh_points and active_mesh: every mesh point assigned to an active region is allowed to emit rays. This model is very time-consuming but is the most accurate. The number of angular points should be reduced when using this model to get a reasonable computation time. 

– box_emitting: every polygon of the .geo file becomes a 3D box for the raytracing program. The spontaneous emission source power from each box is averaged into a hot “spot” of emission and used to emit rays. 

– random_points: Rays are emitted from a randomly-selected points in the device. 

• external_package indicates the existence of an external package that encapsulate the LED. The value of this variable can be chosen as: void, medium, plate or dome. A value of void means the LED is not encapsulated. 

• working_direction defines the working direction of all external packages. It can be chosen as: -x, +x, -y, +y, -z or +z. Figure 23.3 shows how this parameter is defined. 

• device_kind determines what kind of device (led or detector) is being modeled. 

• incident_light_side determines which side of a detector is illuminated. 

• surface_model determines which boundary conditions are applied to certain surfaces; this can be used to represent optical coatings. Note that in certain structures, the coating must be modeled inside the device with the set_3dray_internal_interface statement. 

• surface_model_side determines which side of the device is afected by surface_model. 

• reject_edges determines how the mesh points are used internally by the ray tracing program to average material properties. Choosing yes will cause the program to ignore mesh points too close to box edges which can lead to more a accurate refractive index near large discontinuities. However, it will cause an error if there are not enough mesh points. 

• extract_to_file specifies which file the ray tracing code will export data to; this will be disabled when set to void. The extracted data will be appended to an existing file or will overwrite it, depending on the value of append_data. 

![image](https://cdn-mineru.openxlab.org.cn/result/2026-09-08/f3cc7fbf-2868-424d-9d55-f288d2f2e4bd/7c33c3fd5eca3b4864035419fe0ed07f2ee1ac2ee90832185c16494be38a7286.jpg)


![image](https://cdn-mineru.openxlab.org.cn/result/2026-09-08/f3cc7fbf-2868-424d-9d55-f288d2f2e4bd/252cc69e50d5790a38417b0fdf56ae22405e9d0c972f9ae0405606e382377d04.jpg)


![image](https://cdn-mineru.openxlab.org.cn/result/2026-09-08/f3cc7fbf-2868-424d-9d55-f288d2f2e4bd/e95dd9239c4afd2373924bcad6f56b2e6cfb401b2a1f2bf4fbb4bb3f55068d1b.jpg)



Figure 23.3: An explanation of the packages parameters


• append_data would instruct the program to append data to an existing data file instead of creating a new one. 

• dome_bottom_mirror would set a perfect mirror on the bottom of a dome external package. 

• extract_data specifies which ray tracing result will be extracted to the file specified by extract_to_file. 

• precision is the ray-tracing precision of the ray-tracing calculation. The raytracing procedure is terminated if the relative power of a ray decays to this value. 

• te_portion, tm_portion are the fractions of TE and TM polarizations of propagating light. For non-polarized light te_portion and tm_portion are equal. 

• plate_thickness, plate_width, dome_cyl_height, dome_cyl_radius,dome_sph_radius, dome_base_thickness are geometrical dimensional parameters of the packages. Figure 23.3 illustrates the meaning of all these parameters. They are in units of μm. A given package is built around (or besides) the device. If one or more sizes of a package are set less than device sizes, the program corrects the sizes automatically to their possible minimum values. 

• scale_refl, scale_tran are scaling factors to the Fresnel coeficients which are applied when surface_model=scale_fresnel_coef. 

• thickness1, thickness2, refr_index1,refr_index2, n_layer are the parameters of the multi_layer option of surface_model. 

• package_refr_index defines the complex optical refractive index for all packages. 

• efective_exit_index is the efective index of the coating when surface_model is set to exit_index. 

• datafile_range the range of data sets exported from APSYS/Optowizardused to do the raytracing simulation. Note that this may be diferent than the data sets used for normal plotting purposes. 

• initial_rays is the number of initial rays launched from one emission source point. Equal angular distribution is forced upon these initial rays. 

max_secondary is an integer value parameter, that defines maximum secondary rays generated during multiple reflections and refractions. The raytracing procedure will terminate for a certain initial ray if the number of secondary rays exceeds this number. A ray is regarded as secondary if it is generated by transmission from its initial material box to one of its neighbors. 

```txt
$ No dome do_raytrace_3d precision=0.01 datafile_range=(2,6) initial_rays=8000 
```

• 3d_angle_store is the resolution which is used to store the angular power distribution. It is equal to the number of angular divisions in theta angle and is equal to half of the number of divisions in phi angle. 

• emit_points_number is a total given number of emitting points (“hot spots”) for the plane emission models. 

• max_primary is the maximum number of primary rays allowed. If the primary rays exceeds this number, the program will terminate the tracing of the primary rays. A ray is regarded as primary if it is directly produced from one of the emission points and not from transmission at a material box interface. 

• surface_power_grid is the size of the grid used to record the location and power of rays exiting the device. Six such grids are located around the device to form a box. The resulting surface emission map can be plotted with 3drayplot_surfpower 

• TE_TM_mode defines the behavior of the TE/TM emission model for LEDs. If equal to 0, the mix of TE/TM emission from APSYS is used for the raytracing. For 1, the TE emission spectrum is used and for 2, the TM emission is used. 

## Examples

```txt
rt3d_contact_reflector transp_contact=yes &&
contact1_compindex=(2.
0.5) contact1_thick=0.05 && contact2_compindex=(2.0.5)
contact2_thick=0.05 
```

```txt
$ With dome external package $do_raytrace_3d precision=0.01
datafile_range=(2,6) initial_rays=8000 && $ external_package=dome
working_direction=+y && $ dome_cyl_height=110
dome_base_thickness=10 && $ dome_cyl_radius=500
dome_sph_radius=500 && $ package_refr_index=(1.7,1.0e-6) 
```

## 23.231 dopant_ionization_model

<table><tr><td>parameter</td><td>data type</td><td>values [defaults]</td></tr><tr><td>mott_trans_correction</td><td>char</td><td>[yes],no</td></tr><tr><td>mater_label</td><td>char</td><td></td></tr><tr><td>scale_factor</td><td>real</td><td>[1.]</td></tr><tr><td>max_activation_energy</td><td>real</td><td>[0.1] (eV)</td></tr><tr><td>mater</td><td>intg</td><td>[1]</td></tr></table>

This statement controls the heavy doping efects model for a given material. By default, the Mott transition model is always active for shallow dopants so this command does not need to be issued. See Sec. 5.1.5 for details. 

## Parameters

• mott_trans_correction turns on or of the Mott transition model. 

• scale_factor is a scaling factor for the transition. 

• max_activation_energy defines the maximum ionization energy where the Mott transition is allowed. Deep dopants with ionization energies beyond this value will not be afected by the Mott transition. 

• mater identifies the material afected by this statement. If a label has previously been defined for this material, mater_label may be used instead. 

## Examples

$ To specifically turn off the Mott transition for a material dopant_ionization_model mott_trans_correction=no mater=1 

$ To specifically turn on the Mott transition for a material $ with deep dopants 

dopant_ionization_model max_activation_energy=1e99 mater=2

[defaults]</td></tr><tr><td>impurity</td><td>char</td><td>[shal_dopant]</td></tr><tr><td>charge_type</td><td>char</td><td>[donor],acceptor</td></tr><tr><td>shape</td><td>char</td><td>[gaussian],polygon</td></tr><tr><td>pf_model</td><td>char</td><td>[no],yes</td></tr><tr><td>x_datafile</td><td>char</td><td>[void]</td></tr><tr><td>y_datafile</td><td>char</td><td>[void]</td></tr><tr><td>direction</td><td>char</td><td>[positive],negative</td></tr><tr><td>log_scale</td><td>char</td><td>[no] yes</td></tr><tr><td>xy_datafile</td><td>char</td><td>[void]</td></tr><tr><td>xy-linedata</td><td>char</td><td>[yes]</td></tr><tr><td>traplevel_model</td><td>char</td><td>[void], gaussian, expo_tail, uni-form</td></tr><tr><td>traplevel_tail_side</td><td>char</td><td>[conduction]</td></tr><tr><td>datafile_unit</td><td>char</td><td><eq>[1/m^3],1/cm^3</eq></td></tr><tr><td>sheet_density</td><td>char</td><td>[no] yes</td></tr><tr><td>sheet_location</td><td>char</td><td>[void],bottom,top</td></tr><tr><td>polygon_file</td><td>char</td><td>[void]</td></tr><tr><td>all_segment</td><td>char</td><td>[no]</td></tr><tr><td>polygon_z_datafile</td><td>char</td><td>[void]</td></tr><tr><td>polygon_z_datafile_unit</td><td>char</td><td><eq>[1/m^3],1/cm^3</eq></td></tr><tr><td>polygon_z_datafile_log</td><td>char</td><td>[yes]</td></tr><tr><td>decay_type</td><td>char</td><td>[gaussian],erfc</td></tr><tr><td>use_position_label</td><td>char</td><td>[no], yes</td></tr><tr><td>x1_label</td><td>char</td><td>[void]</td></tr><tr><td>x2_label</td><td>char</td><td>[void]</td></tr><tr><td>y1_label</td><td>char</td><td>[void]</td></tr><tr><td>y2_label</td><td>char</td><td>[void]</td></tr><tr><td>max_conc</td><td>real</td><td><eq>1.e10 (m^{-3})</eq></td></tr><tr><td>level</td><td>real</td><td>[0.01] (eV)</td></tr><tr><td>x_prof</td><td>realx4</td><td><eq>x1,x2,dx1,dx2 (\mu m)</eq></td></tr><tr><td>y_prof</td><td>realx4</td><td><eq>y1,y2,dy1,dy2 (\mu m)</eq></td></tr><tr><td>edge1_prof</td><td>realx3</td><td><eq>x2,y2,dx2</eq></td></tr><tr><td>edge2_prof</td><td>realx3</td><td><eq>x2,y2,dx2</eq></td></tr><tr><td>edge3_prof</td><td>realx3</td><td><eq>x3,y3,dx3</eq></td></tr><tr><td>edge4_prof</td><td>realx3</td><td><eq>x4,y4,dx4</eq></td></tr><tr><td>pf_cont_shift</td><td>real</td><td>[0.] (eV)</td></tr><tr><td>arc_height1</td><td>real</td><td><eq>[0.](\mu m)</eq></td></tr><tr><td>arc_height2</td><td>real</td><td><eq>[0.](\mu m)</eq></td></tr><tr><td>arc_height3</td><td>real</td><td><eq>[0.](\mu m)</eq></td></tr><tr><td>arc_height4</td><td>real</td><td><eq>[0.](\mu m)</eq></td></tr><tr><td>origin</td><td>real</td><td>[0.] (μm)</td></tr><tr><td>grading_from</td><td>real</td><td>(fraction)</td></tr><tr><td>grading_angle</td><td>real</td><td>[90.] (degrees)</td></tr><tr><td>xy_data_factor</td><td>real</td><td>[1.]</td></tr><tr><td>xy_x_transform</td><td>realx2</td><td>[1. 0.]</td></tr><tr><td>xy_y_transform</td><td>realx2</td><td>[1. 0.]</td></tr><tr><td>traplevel_stddev</td><td>real</td><td>[0.1](eV)</td></tr><tr><td>traplevel_tail</td><td>real</td><td>[0.05](eV)</td></tr><tr><td>label_tail</td><td>real</td><td>[0.01] (μm)</td></tr><tr><td>polygon_file_stddev</td><td>real</td><td>[0.01] (μm)</td></tr><tr><td>z_stddev</td><td>real</td><td>[0.] (μm)</td></tr><tr><td>z_stddev2</td><td>real</td><td>(μm)</td></tr><tr><td>pnt_num</td><td>intg</td><td>[4]</td></tr><tr><td>newdoping_index</td><td>intg</td><td></td></tr><tr><td>xy_skipline</td><td>intg</td><td>[1]</td></tr><tr><td>xy_data_column</td><td>intg</td><td>[3]</td></tr></table>

The statement doping is a frequently used statement which describes the doping profile of impurities or impurity concentration distribution. This statement is often generated automatically when processing the .layer file and should only be used by advanced users. 

Multiple doping statements covering overlapping areas can be used to define codoping or create advanced shapes. Experimental or doping profiles simulated in an another program can also be defined using this statement. 

For an even more accurate model, the user is encouraged to try our CSUPREM software which can model the entire growth process of semiconductor devices including etching, difusion and implantation. 

## Parameters

• The parameter impurity defines the type of impurity being modeled: 

– shal_dopant or shal_dopanti,i=1..9 for shallow dopants; multiple species may be defined 

– trap_i,i=1..9 for deep level traps of type i. By default, trap species #1 is the intrinsic trap density of the material, correponding to the lifetime_n and lifetime_p macros. Defining these trap properties directly overrides the lifetime values from the macro; more specifically, the capture cross-sections corresponding to these lifetimes. To specify a trap that provides an additional recombination mechanism beyond what’s intrinsic to the material quality itself, use additional trap species (#2 or above) in order to leave the original lifetime settings intact. 

– fix_charge for fixed space charges 

When defining traps, the user should also define the statements trap_ncap_i and trap_pcap_i in the .sol file to specify the capture cross sections of the trap. 

• charge_type is the charge state of the impurity. For shallow dopants and traps, it must be either donor or acceptor. For fixed space charges, it must be positive or negative. 

• shape controls the shape of the doping region created by this statement: 

– For gaussian or rectangle, the profile is uniform within a rectangle (see Fig. 23.4) and falls of from the sides using Gaussian tails. The corner points and standard deviations for the Gaussian tails are given by using x_prof and y_prof. 

The 4 values in x_prof correspond to (x1,x2,dx1,dx2) (in μm). y_prof similarly defines (y1,y2,dy1,dy2). Note that if x1=x2 and y1=y2, only the tails remain and the shape collapses into a standard Gaussian. 

Alternatively, use_position_label allows the use of the position labels in x1_label, x2_label, y1_label and y2_label to define the corner points. In such a case, the tail value is set using label_tail. 

– polygon extends the above behavior with uniform doping inside a more generic polygon with corner points defined through edgei_prof,i=1..4. These commands each define 3 values(x,y,dx); (x,y) are the corner coordinates for point i and dx is the standard deviation of the Gaussian tail extending perpendicularly from the polygon edge connecting points i and i+1. 

The number of corner points actually used to define the polygon is controlled with pnt_num (see Fig. 23.5). 

– use_polygon_file further extends the above behavior with a uniform doping with a polygon whose corners are defined inside a separate text file (polygon_file). As before, each edge of the polygon has a Gaussian tail extending perpendicular to it; the standard deviation of this tail is defined in polygon_file_stddev. 

Note that when importing doping profiles (e.g. SIMS) from a data file, the shape parameter should be left to its default values. 

• decay_type can be used to replace the standard Gaussian tails mentioned throughout this section with the complimentary error function (erfc). 

• pf_model turns on/of the Poole-Frenkel model for field-induced thermionic emission of partially ionized dopants. Such a model is important when the ionization energy is high or when the operating temperature is low. 

• x_datafile or y_datafile allows the user to import numerical doping data for the x or y direction. The file data must be stored in 2-column format: 

relative_distance_in_microns doping_density (no blank lines anywhere) 

The doping density stored inside the file can be in linear or log10 scale depending on the choice of log_scale. The units of the data can also be set by datafile_unit (1/m<sup>3</sup> or 1/cm<sup>3</sup>). 

The exact position of the doping when importing a profile is further controlled by two other parameters: 

– direction is used to flip the sign of the coordinates for imported data files. If negative, the actual doping profile is in a direction opposite to the relative-distance given in the data file. 

– origin is the origin used to calculate the absolute position for the data points of the imported doping profile. 

The actual coordinates used in device simulation are therefore: 

(origin) + (sign-of-direction)*(relative-distance) 

• sheet_density instructs the program that the present command is used to describe a sheet density profile instead of the bulk default. The max_conc would be in unit of 1/m<sup>2</sup> to define a sheet charge over the x-y plane. 

• sheet_location is used in conjunction with sheet_density=yes for a 3D structure. It defines the location of the sheet charge either on the top or bottom of the present z-segment. 

• max_conc defines the maximum concentration of the doping profile. 

• level defines the relative energy level of the impurity. For shallow donors and deep level traps, the level is calculated from the conduction band. For shallow acceptors the level is measured from the valence band. For gaussian type of trap model, this parameter also refers to the maximum trap level position. 

Note that this parameter is used to describe the energy level of the shallow or deep impurities over the whole device. If the properties of the impurity in each diferent material need to be defined, the user must override this value with one of the following statements: 

– shal_dnr_level 

– shal_dnr_level_i 

– shal_acpt_level 

– shal_acpt_level_i 

– trap_level_i 

• pf_cont_shift is the ionization energy level shift (due to the Poole-Frenkel efect) at the contact. Once it is specified by this parameter, the ionization energy is fixed during the simulation while the shift in the bulk material is varied as a function of the local electric field. 

• arc_heighti,i=1..4 are parameters are used to bend the edge of the doping profile given in edgei_prof. The values of arc_height can take positive or negative numbers. The sign of the height is understood as follows: 

Let the counter-clockwise direction of the vector defined by two adjacent points of the polygon be vector e and the vector pointing from the starting point of e to a point on the arc be vector a. Then a positive height means that the cross product a cross e must point to positive z direction. On the other hand, if the height is negative, the cross product yields a vector in the negative z direction. 

In other words, a positive height makes the edge curve outwards while a negative height makes the edge curve inwards. These parameters can be used together with the edge_curve statement. 

• grading_from, if used, is the relative starting value of a linearly graded doping profile; its value is relative to the final doping value of max_conc. This linear grading is in the direction specified with grading_angle where the angle definition follows the usual convention (0 degrees = +x direction). 

• newdoping_index, if set to positive integer, is used to label a doping profile so that the doping concentration of this profile may be varied during a simulation. The variable key word in the scan statement to control this profile is new_doping. 

• xy_datafile, xy_linedata, xy_data_factor, xy_x_transform, xy_y_transform, xy_skipline, xy_data_column. 

All these parameters with the xy_ prefix are used to import 2D doping profiles in ASCII format: 

– xy_datafile is doping data file in a column-wise format: 

```txt
x y doping-concentration 
```

where x and y are in micons and doping-concentration is in 1/m<sup>3</sup>. To convert to a diferent unit, the parameter xy_data_factor may be used. 

– xy_linedata indicates whether the doping profile is line-ordered data or randomly distributed. If yes, the data is ordered in multiple straight x-lines, from left to right. The x-lines are ordered from bottom to top. Using random data generally yields a less accurate profile after interpolation. 

– xy_data_factor is a multiplication factor applied to the doping concentration. It may also be used as a way of converting units. 

– xy_x_transform consists two parameters (say a and b) which can be used to perform a linear transform of the x-coordinate in the doping profile so that the actual coordinate used by the simulator is a*x+b. 

– xy_y_transform is the same as xy_x_transform except it is for y-coordinate. 

– xy_skipline is the number of header lines in the doping file that should be skipped. 

– xy_data_column is the data column number where the doping-concentration can be found. In some data files, n- and p-doping are stored in diferent columns and this parameter can be used to specify which type of doping is to be used from the same file. 

The following code is used internally to read the doping profile and may be helpful in understanding the required syntax: 

```fortran
! program skips lines of xy_skipline first. then, the following code
! is used
    do jj=1,npnt
    read(iu,*) (adata(kk),kk=1,jcolumn)
! this include unit change, direction flip, etc., and shift ref. point
! let me assume unit is um
    xdata(jj)=adata(1)*xy_x_transform1+xy_x_transform2
    ydata(jj)=adata(2)*xy_y_transform1+xy_y_transform2
! unit here is 1/m**3 
```

```txt
if(log_flag.eq.0) then
ddata(jj)=adata(jcolumn)*xy_data_factor
else
ddata(jj)=10.d0**(adata(jcolumn))*xy_data_factor
endif
enddo 
```

• traplevel_model may be used to define a continuous energy distribution of trap states. If this parameter is not used, then a single energy level is used. 

For exponential tail models, the trap density of states is proprotional to either $e ^ { - ( E _ { c } - E ) / L } ~ \mathrm { o r } ~ e ^ { - ( E - E _ { v } ) / L }$ , depending on the value of traplevel_tail_side. The decay parameter L is defined by traplevel_tail. 

When using exponential tails, the concept of “trap level” is not relevant as the distribution does not need to be centered. The traplevel_tail_side setting therefore controls whether the exponential tail starts at the conduction band edge or the valence band edge: in both cases, the tail decays towards the middle of the bandgap. 

For Gaussian models, the trap density of states is proportional to $e ^ { - \frac { ( E - E _ { t } ) ^ { 2 } } { 2 \sigma ^ { 2 } } }$ where $E _ { t }$ is set through the level parameter. The trap level standard deviation σ is set through the traplevel_stddev parameter. 

For uniform trap models the energy distribution is centered around the level parameter, with a width set by traplevel_width. 

• all_segment instructs the software to apply the 2D doping profile defined by this command to all mesh planes of all z-segments. 

• polygon_z_datafile is used to extend the 2D doping profile across multiple mesh planes. The file specified in this command must contain 2 columns: the z-position and the maximum 2D doping concentration at that position. Units and log-scaling for this file are controlled by polygon_z_datafile_unit and polygon_z_datafile_log; these parameters follow the same rules as datafile_unit and log_scale, respectively. 

• z_stddev extends the 2D doping profile in the z-direction so that it straddles multiple mesh planes. It defines a Gaussian tail which is applied in both directions. For an asymmetric Gaussian profile in z, z_stddev2 may also be defined. 

## Examples

doping impurity=shal_dopant charge_type=donor max_conc=1.e24 && 

![image](https://cdn-mineru.openxlab.org.cn/result/2026-09-08/f3cc7fbf-2868-424d-9d55-f288d2f2e4bd/6db5afba81ce7368aedaf85518db753132e09bd2eebb63c76b2006c3cb7165ca.jpg)


Figure 23.4: Schematics for the Gaussian/Rectangle doping profile in the xdirection. 

```txt
x_prof=(0.0e0, 10.0e0, 0.01e0, 0.01e0) &&
y_prof= 0.e0, 1.4e0, 0.02e0, 0.02e0

doping impurity=trap_1 charge_type=acceptor max_conc=1.e22 &&
x_prof=(0.0e0, 10.0e0, 0.01e0, 0.01e0) &&
y_prof=(0.e0, 10.e0, 0.02e0, 0.02e0)

doping impurity=shal_dopant charge_type=donor max_conc=1.e24 &&
shape=polygon &&
edge1_prof= (0. 1.1 0.01) &&
edge2_prof= (0.429 1.1 0.01) &&
edge3_prof= (0.146 1.5 0.01) &&
edge4_prof= (0. 1.5 0.01)

doping impurity=shal_dopant charge_type=acceptor max_conc=1.5e23 &&
level=0.115 pf_model=yes pf_cont_shift=0.115 &&
x_prof=(0.0e0, 10.0e0, 0.01e0, 0.01e0) &&
y_prof=(0.e0, 1.e0, 0.02e0, 0.02e0) 
```

![image](https://cdn-mineru.openxlab.org.cn/result/2026-09-08/f3cc7fbf-2868-424d-9d55-f288d2f2e4bd/27d718b8a195b284a88196269a1eb4aeaf48d45df587636cd30a318143d72352.jpg)



Figure 23.5: Schematics for the Polygon doping profile. Note the labeling of points and corresponding edges.


## 23.233 double_mesh

<table><tr><td>parameter</td><td>data type</td><td>values [defaults]</td></tr><tr><td colspan="3"></td></tr><tr><td>mesh_inf</td><td>char</td><td></td></tr><tr><td>mesh_outf</td><td>char</td><td></td></tr><tr><td>polygon</td><td>char</td><td></td></tr><tr><td>order</td><td>char</td><td>[yes],no</td></tr><tr><td>direction</td><td>intg</td><td>1,2</td></tr><tr><td>range_1</td><td>realx2</td><td>(um)</td></tr><tr><td>range_2</td><td>realx2</td><td>(um)</td></tr></table>

The mesh generation statement double_mesh provides the basic mechanism to refine a rough mesh manually. It can be used to double the existing mesh density in a given region in the device. This statement is rarely needed because mesh can be automatically refined using the refine_mesh statement. 

## Parameters

• mesh_inf is the mesh input file. 

• mesh_outf is the mesh output file. 

• polygon is the name of the polygon afected by this statement. 

• order specifies the format of the mesh output. If the mesh output is to be used by the solver, yes should be used, i.e., ordering of the mesh is required to interface with the solver. If the mesh is to be further manipulated after this statement, no should be used. 

![image](https://cdn-mineru.openxlab.org.cn/result/2026-09-08/f3cc7fbf-2868-424d-9d55-f288d2f2e4bd/74371ae3c26ab78d220f293158d036585b3e1097f15497700be0b710ef33a7a2.jpg)



Figure 23.6: Schematics for the region to be manually refined.


• direction is the direction of the mesh lines to be doubled. Direction 1 corresponds to lines parallel to the first edge of the polygon; direction 2 refers to lines parallel to the second edge. See Fig. 23.6 for details. 

• range_1 defines the range along direction 1 in which the mesh doubling will occur. This distance is measured on the first edge of the polygon. 

• range_2 is the same as range_1 excepts that it works along direction 2. 

## Examples

```txt
double_mesh polygon=p1 direction=1 && range_1 = (0.1 0.5) range_2=(0.6 0.9) && mesh_inf=cas1.msh1 mesh_outf=cas1.msh2 order=yes 
```

## 23.234 dox_efield0_pf_elec

<table><tr><td>parameter</td><td>data type</td><td>values [defaults]</td></tr><tr><td>(see) material_par</td><td></td><td></td></tr></table>

The material statement dox_efield0_pf_elec is a constant $E 0 _ { p f }$ (in V/m) used to define the Poole-Frenkel-like E-field dependent electron mobility: $\mu _ { n } = \mu _ { 0 } e x p [ \sqrt ( E / E 0 _ { p f } ) ]$ This parameter is used to describe a doped organic (thus the prefix dox) material. For more details, please see the following reference: 

B. Ryhstaller and S.A. Carter, S. Barth, H. Riel, and W. Riess, "Transient and steadystate behavior of space charges in multilayer organic light-emitting diodes," J. Appl. Phys., 15 April, 2001, Vol. 89, No. 8, pp. 4575-4586. 

The parameters for this statement are the same as for all other material statements. The use of these parameters and related examples are given under material_par in section 23.470. 

Example(s) 

dox_efield0_pf_elec value=1.959e8 mater=1 

## 23.235 dox_efield0_pf_hole

<table><tr><td>parameter</td><td>data type</td><td>values [defaults]</td></tr><tr><td>(see) material_par</td><td></td><td></td></tr></table>

The material statement dox_efield0_pf_hole is a constant $E 0 _ { p f }$ (in V/m) used to define the Poole-Frenkel-like E-field dependent hole mobility: $\mu _ { p } = \mu _ { 0 } e x p [ \sqrt ( E / E 0 _ { p f } ) ]$ This parameter is used to describe a doped organic (thus the prefix dox) material. For more details, please see the following reference: 

B. Ryhstaller and S.A. Carter, S. Barth, H. Riel, and W. Riess, "Transient and steadystate behavior of space charges in multilayer organic light-emitting diodes," J. Appl. Phys., 15 April, 2001, Vol. 89, No. 8, pp. 4575-4586. 

The parameters for this statement are the same as for all other material statements. The use of these parameters and related examples are given under material_par in section 23.470. 

Example(s) 

dox_efield0_pf_hole value=1.959e8 mater=1 

## 23.236 dox_el_weight

<table><tr><td>parameter</td><td>data type</td><td>values [defaults]</td></tr><tr><td>(see) material_par</td><td></td><td></td></tr></table>

The material statement dox_el_weight is used when there are light-emitting dopants in an organic semiconductor. In that case, we must define the relative contribution from host material and the dopants. 

This command defines the weighing factor for the primary dopant; see also ox_el_weight for the contribution from host material. Additional weights from other dopants may be included by commands named doxj_el_weight wherej=2...5 

To compute the EL spectrum when such dopants are present, two approaches are used depending on whether or not the exciton difusion equations are solved. If exciton transport is enabled by using the organic_exciton_dif command, dopant and host spectra at each mesh point are simply mixed by local exciton density, divided by their respective lifetimes, and weighed by this factor. If exciton transport is not considered, their respective bimolecular recombination rates are used along with this factor to determine the relative contributions. 

The parameters for this statement are the same as for all other material statements. See material_par in section 23.470 for examples and further details. 

## Examples

dox_el_weight value=0.01 mater=1 ox_el_weight value=1 mater=1 

## 23.237 dox_exciton_eg

This statement is the same as ox_exciton_eg except it is for the dopant material. 

## 23.238 dox_extern_spectrum

<table><tr><td>parameter</td><td>data type</td><td>values [defaults]</td></tr><tr><td>(see) material_par</td><td></td><td></td></tr></table>

The material statement dox_extern_spectrum is used when there are light-emitting dopants in an organic semiconductor. In that case, we must define the emission spectrum for this dopant. It is often convenient to define this quantity by loading experimental data from a table or by defining a user function. 

This command therefore re-uses the same functionality as the rest of the macro system but it is mandatory to have the reserved keyword wavelength as an input variable; this is always defined in micrometers. If the table format is used, the second column will correspond to the EL spectrum: its units are arbitrary as the program will normalize the spectrum to match the expected value for the total emission rate. 

Note that this command defines values for the primary dopant. See also ox_extern_spectrum for the host material and doxj_extern_spectrum, j=2...5 for other dopants that may be present. 

The parameters for this statement are the same as for all other material statements. See material_par in section 23.470 for examples and further details. 

## Examples

```txt
dox_extern_spectrum variation=table
table(wavelength)
0.488524E+00 0.309522E-01
0.499601E+00 0.421672E-01
...
end_table 
```

This statement is the same as ox_exciton_eg except it is for the dopant material.

This statement is the same as dox_gaussian_sdj except it is for the dopant material.

This statement is the same as ox_hopping_energy except it is for the dopant material.

This statement is the same as dox_peak_abs except it is for the dopant material. 

## 23.243 dox_vib_quanta

This statement is the same as ox_vib_quanta except it is for the dopant material. 

## 23.244 dox_xp_coupling

This statement is the same as dox_xp_coupling except it is for the dopant material.

See dox_el_weight.

## 23.253 edge_curve

<table><tr><td>parameter</td><td>data type</td><td>values [defaults]</td></tr><tr><td colspan="3"></td></tr><tr><td>edge</td><td>charx2</td><td></td></tr><tr><td>shape</td><td>char</td><td>[arc]</td></tr><tr><td>height</td><td></td><td>(μm)</td></tr></table>

This mesh generation statement bends the edge of a polygon into a specific shape. 

## Parameters

• edge contains the two point labels describing the edge to be bent. 

• shape is the shape into which the edge is to be bent. Currently, only arc is supported. 

• height is the height of the arc. 

This value is signed and can be understood by drawing two vectors: $\overrightarrow { v _ { 1 } } ,$ which follows the two points in edge and $\overrightarrow { v _ { 2 } } ^ { - }$ which goes from the first specified point in edge to a new point at the top of the arc. If the two points in edge follow the usual positive (i.e. counter-clockwise) direction, then $\overrightarrow { v _ { 1 } } \times \overrightarrow { v _ { 2 } }$ will be positive. The height vector is parallel to this cross product and share the same sign. 

In other words, as long as the two points in edge are ordered counterclockwise, a positive height value produces a convex bend while a negative value produces a concave bend. 

## Examples

edge_curve edge=(a b) shape=arc height=-0.3 

## 23.254 eeim_optic

<table><tr><td>parameter</td><td>data type</td><td>values [defaults]</td></tr><tr><td colspan="3"></td></tr><tr><td>gain_correction</td><td>char</td><td>[yes],no</td></tr><tr><td>sort_imag</td><td>char</td><td>[yes],no</td></tr><tr><td>pure_index_loss</td><td>char</td><td>[yes],no</td></tr><tr><td>export_eeimmsg</td><td>char</td><td>[no],yes</td></tr><tr><td>modal_base</td><td>intg</td><td>[3]</td></tr><tr><td>xsearch_real</td><td>intg</td><td>[2000]</td></tr><tr><td>xsearch_imag</td><td>intg</td><td>[40]</td></tr><tr><td>muller_maxit</td><td>intg</td><td>[80]</td></tr><tr><td>wi (i=1,2,3...19)</td><td>real</td><td>(um)</td></tr><tr><td>xsearch_range</td><td>real</td><td>[5.]</td></tr><tr><td>muller_vartol</td><td>real</td><td>[1.e-8]</td></tr><tr><td>muller_functol</td><td>real</td><td>[1.e-5]</td></tr></table>

The eeim_optic statement is used to invoke the enhanced efective index method (EEIM) for the optical mode computation. It can be used to treat device structure with leaky (radiative) modes. 

The rectangle solution area is defined by point_ll and point_ur in the init_wave statement. It assumes that the modes in the y-direction is confined. The boundary on the right is such that it can support traveling wave to the right (which may be decaying as in the case of confined modes). 

The boundary on the left can be confined as even or odd, or as radiative. The left boundary may be modified using the first number (we shall refer to as lx1) in boundary_type of the init_wave statement. If lx1 is 1, the left boundary is to set the wave to zero (odd). If lx1 is 2, the left boundary is to set the wave derivative to zero (even). If lx1 is 3, the left boundary is to have traveling wave going out of the device (to the left). 

To study device with radiation loss to the substrate, we suggest that the device be rotated by 90 degrees so that the substrate is towards the +x direction. The rotated device may be done within the .geo file or by using the GeoEditor. 

• gain_correction may be used to enable the optical gain in the rate equation to be corrected for radiation loss. With this setting to “no”, The simulator will calculate the modal gain by averaging the local material gain with weight of the wave intensity. 

sort_imag is used to decide if EEIM modes are sorted according to the imaginary parts of the optical dielectric constant. If positive, the modes will be sorted according to modal gain with the highest first. If negative, the real part will be sorted with the highest real index first. 

pure_index_loss. There are two ways to estimate the modal radiative loss. The first is the pure index method. This is by setting all imaginary material indices to zero throughout the device. The imaginary part of the eigen value of the EEIM is the modal loss. The other method is to estimate the bias dependent radiative loss by comparing the EEIM solution with averaged material gain. The former is a convenient and definite way of characterizing the device. The latter is more realistic but less stable numerically. Please note that if pure_index_loss=yes, only the modal radiative loss for pure index structure is printed as run-time message. If pure_index_loss=no, both the radiative loss for pure index structure and the bias-dependent radiative loss at diferent bias will be printed 

• export_eeimmsg is used to enable the export of EEIM column index data in .sol.msg files. 

• modal_base is the number of y-modes within each column. In principle, there are infinite number of y-modes is the mode searching range is large enough. In practice, several of these will be suficient. 

• xsearch_real is the number of search points for the x-modes in the real part of ${ \overline { { \varepsilon } } } ,$ the eigen solution of the x-modes. 

• xsearch_imag is the number of search points for the x-modes in the imaginary part of ε, the eigen solution of the x-modes. 

• muller_maxit is the maximum number of the Muller solver that is used to find the complex root as the eigen solution of the lateral modes. 

• wi $\scriptstyle ( \mathbf { i = 1 } , 2 , 3 , . . . , 1 9 )$ is the ith column width. 

• xsearch_range is the search range for the real part of ε, the eigen solution of the x-modes. 

• muller_vartol is the variable relative tolerance at convergence of the Muller solver. 

• muller_functol is the function relative tolerance at convergence of the Muller solver. 

## Examples

```txt
eeim_optic modal_base=3 && w1=2 w2=0.75 w3=1.25 && xsearch_real=2000 xsearch_imag=5 
```

The above statement uses three y-modes, sets the with for each column. The number search points are also specified. 

## 23.255 efective_medium

<table><tr><td>parameter</td><td>data type</td><td>values [defaults]</td></tr><tr><td colspan="3"></td></tr><tr><td>materi(i=1..29)</td><td>intg</td><td></td></tr><tr><td>xrange</td><td>realx2</td><td>(um)</td></tr><tr><td>yrange</td><td>realx2</td><td>(um)</td></tr><tr><td>zrange</td><td>realx2</td><td>(um)</td></tr></table>

efective_medium defines an efective “average” material out of multiple bulk materials. This is useful in regions like DBR layers where multiple thin layers are needed for optical modeling but the electrical modeling can be approximated by a single bulk materials. The software will compute the afinity, bandgap, DOS, etc... based on a weighed average of the materials present in the specified region. 

For a similar model used in QWs, please see efective_miniband_model. 

## Parameters

• materi(i=1..29) are the material numbers that are considered when constructing the efective medium. 

• xrange, yrange and zrange are the extent of the efective medium. 

## Examples

effective_medium mater1=1 mater2=2 yrange=(2.5 2.75) 

## 23.256 efective_miniband_model

<table><tr><td>parameter</td><td>data type</td><td>values [defaults]</td></tr><tr><td colspan="3"></td></tr><tr><td>materi(i=1..9)</td><td>intg</td><td></td></tr><tr><td>xrange</td><td>realx2</td><td>(um)</td></tr><tr><td>yrange</td><td>realx2</td><td>(um)</td></tr><tr><td>zrange</td><td>realx2</td><td>(um)</td></tr><tr><td>set_cond_level</td><td>real</td><td>(ev)</td></tr><tr><td>set_val_level</td><td>real</td><td>(ev)</td></tr></table>

efective_miniband_model defines an efective miniband model for coupled quantum wells/dots. For the specified materials, the solver creates an efective band structure with band edges defined by the miniband levels and density of states from the quantum mechanical modeling. 

For bulk layers, please see efective_medium. 

## Parameters

• materi(i=1..9) are the material numbers assigned to the miniband. 

• xrange, yrange and zrange are the extent of the miniband. 

• set_cond_level and set_val_level can be used to manually override the position of the miniband energy levels. The values are measured from the QW bottom. 

## Examples

effective_miniband_model mater1=1 mater2=2 yrange=(2.5 2.75) 

## 23.257 efield0_pf_elec

<table><tr><td>parameter</td><td>data type</td><td>values [defaults]</td></tr><tr><td>(see) material_par</td><td></td><td></td></tr></table>

The material statement efield0_pf_elec is a constant $E 0 _ { p f } ( \mathrm { i n V / m } )$ used to define the Poole-Frenkel-like E-field dependent electron mobility: $\mu _ { n } = \mu _ { 0 } e x p [ \sqrt ( E / E 0 _ { p f } ) ]$ For more details, please see the following reference: 

B. Ryhstaller and S.A. Carter, S. Barth, H. Riel, and W. Riess, "Transient and steadystate behavior of space charges in multilayer organic light-emitting diodes," J. Appl. Phys., 15 April, 2001, Vol. 89, No. 8, pp. 4575-4586. 

The parameters for this statement are the same as for all other material statements. The use of these parameters and related examples are given under material_par in section 23.470. 

Example(s) 

efield0_pf_elec value=1.959e8 mater=1 

## 23.258 efield0_pf_hole

<table><tr><td>parameter</td><td>data type</td><td>values [defaults]</td></tr><tr><td>(see) material_par</td><td></td><td></td></tr></table>

The material statement efield0_pf_hole is a constant $E 0 _ { p f }$ (in V/m) used to define the Poole-Frenkel-like E-field dependent hole mobility: $\mu _ { p } = \mu _ { 0 } e x p [ \sqrt ( E / E 0 _ { p f } ) ]$ For more details, please see the following reference: 

B. Ryhstaller and S.A. Carter, S. Barth, H. Riel, and W. Riess, "Transient and steadystate behavior of space charges in multilayer organic light-emitting diodes," J. Appl. Phys., 15 April, 2001, Vol. 89, No. 8, pp. 4575-4586. 

The parameters for this statement are the same as for all other material statements. The use of these parameters and related examples are given under material_par in section 23.470.
